Thirdly, Of course, we will also have the Schengen border after the 1st century. May maintain in full male and female strength to ensure that no one can enter Austrian soil unjustly in the future. But we get a much better protected EU external border at the borders of Poland, at the borders of Slovakia, Hungary and Slovenia. This means a second “safety belt” for the safety of Austria. Fourth: We want to bring ourselves closer to the European Union than before. The recently adopted asylum procedure in Europe shows that the Austrian path is fully recognised, supported and implemented by the European Member States.

However, what can be calculated directly – as one does not have to wait for the future – are the incidence effects of this tax reform, i.e. the impact of the tax changes on distribution. We have already heard a lot about the human incidence, particularly by not raising the negative tax, and, taking into account the cold progression, there has hardly been a shift in the distribution curve here. But I think we should look at the functional distribution of income, i.e. the distribution of the effects on the factors of production. If you start from the financial science approach, it is quite clear that the concept of a reform – that is, a tax reform – requires a change in the tax structure so that you can even call it “reform”. And, please, a change in the tax structure has not taken place with this tax reform, because in absolute figures the reduction of profits is just as high as the reduction of wage income, only: As far as wage incomes so far contribute a much greater share to the tax than profits, this imbalance naturally continues to shift at the expense of the labour factor. It fits well in the picture that all investment benefits will end this year. That is, what is happening here is a general relief of profits, but not a relief of profit use. And that's how it's going to be. You cannot economically achieve the effects you want. If you do not favour profit-use, especially domestic profit-use, it may well happen – and this is not only in line with the usual economic theory, but we have enough practical examples from other countries – that with these measures you can even relocate abroad, because you generally relieve profits, foreign investment will of course be easier. Doralt, too, in this context, I would also like to mention that I did not fully understand that Professor Doralt was also attacked by representatives of the ÖVP, and not only by those of the FPÖ, of which I expected this, when hearing in his reputation as a scientist, but whether it was about it – has confirmed that Austria is now the final highlight of the investment benefits. That is, this multiplier effect of 0.25, which you obviously aim at here, can easily be simulated. One last word on the Lisbon strategy. As it is always stressed that this tax reform would also be in line with the EU's Lisbon strategy, I would like to say that: She doesn't do that! The Lisbon strategy requires additional investment in the area of human capital, a relief of the labour factor and, if it is to be implemented properly, an end to the tax competition. – Do in all three areas You're the exact opposite! Lisbon requires a shift in the tax burden from labour to capital – and thus the opposite of this tax reform. That means you have to change your European policy as well. What needs to be done is an enormously stronger coordination in the tax field, a genuine European capital income tax and a fiscal strategy for the anticyclic stimulation of private capital. This is our European policy, and therefore we are not afraid of the forthcoming European elections. Thank you.

These credits are partly much higher than the basic content. In the individual companies, the amount of the credits is based on how much capital is distributed to the owners. If we in Germany have a tax rate of more than 30 percent, in the new candidate countries, we have tax rates between 16 and 19 percent, but we are 34 percent, i.e. double taxation, then we have to lower our tax rate if we do not want to lose jobs. That is exactly the reason for this offensive strategy. We believe that this has made a contribution to tax harmonisation in Central Europe and thus also in Europe at all. This cannot happen from one country to another, but must be done gradually. In short, something that seems particularly important to me. I mean, the essential thing about this tax reform is not just that people have net more in their pockets – it's a real tax reduction, a net tax reduction! – but also that this tax reduction is based on an economic dynamic which not only affects Austria but also strengthens our position in Central Europe. I therefore believe, and I would like to tell Mr Eder that now is exactly the time for this. A few days after the new countries have joined, we set the sign. We lower taxes, we are competitive to the east and to the west, to the north and to the south. And I can tell you: Those people who find work will be grateful for it.

This is a closed and coordinated approach to this dumping in Brussels. These are essentially the points that are important. We also want the tax system to be substantially simplified. ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-06-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e20748	We're now on the 3rd. Agenda item. We are immediately entering the debate. ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-06-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e20773	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The Socialist Group would have liked to accept this bill, but it would have been necessary for a resolution that we had prepared to be accepted by the government parties. Unfortunately, that was not the case. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The Product Piracy Act implements, on the one hand, the provisions of the EU Product Piracy Regulation, there are new implementing provisions, and on the other hand, the customs authorities' powers to combat piracy. Product piracy is about intellectual property rights. But this is where we see the problems, because there is a risk that the so-called right holders, namely the patent holders, will misuse the rights of this abuse. There has already been a case in Brussels in which Monsanto has tried to force the European customs authorities and customs authorities to control all other products for competition reasons alone, so that Monsanto can obtain the relevant information. The crucial problem in the case of such injuries lies with the grounds of claim and with the sanctions of the piracy of products. There is no agreement on this today. We are only voting today on a so-called enforcement law for the customs authorities. The main problems lie in patent law, another problem in trademark protection law and in variety law. ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-06-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e20980	Next speaker is Mrs Rester-Hinterseer. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2004-05-06-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00059_d7e20999	Mr President. Mr. Minister! With my previous speaker I had to hear a certain contradiction, because on the one hand, the colleague said that in the face of complicated matter there should not be any hip-shot actions, but on the other hand, the fact is that the Committee had a real debate on this and that the meeting had to be interrupted, because the colleagues present at ÖVP and FPÖ did not know exactly what was actually decided on by this law. Let me pick up something on the subject of patents, because of course this has something to do with intellectual property issues. The Agreement on Trade-related Aspects of Intellectual Property Rights, which is called short trips and is part of the WTO Treaties, is one of the most controversial international treaties in recent years. In contrast to other WTO provisions, the aim is not to facilitate trade and free trade, but to establish high-level protection systems. There is no objection to this, because it is in fact necessary to protect brands that have worked out companies with a high level of effort. In our view, however, we all still assume that a patent can be applied for to process techniques for the production of products according to national view. This patent is then valid for 20 years. In the TRIPS, however, it is established that not only the process techniques, but also the product itself is protected by means of a so-called patent. The manufacturer, the inventor so to speak, can not only protect the techniques of the process, but can also protect all production and use possibilities. What does that mean? Let me tell you this on the basis of a specific case. Example: US-American company DuPont. This company has been issued by the European Patent Office in Munich to the EP 744 888 patent, which includes all maize plants that exceed a certain proportion of oil and oleic acid. However, it is not about genetically bred maize, but about seed produced under quite traditional breeding conditions. So that means that DuPont not only protected his own maize, but all the maize varieties that contain genetically exactly the proportion that her maize, which is protected, contains. The same with wheat and the group Monsanto. This time, a patent for wheat was granted for the production of crispy, flour-containing edible products such as biscuits or similar products, i.e. also by the Munich Patent Office. In fact, the researchers at Monsanto have done nothing but the farmers and breeders before them, they have specifically crossed certain plant varieties together to get this result. And with that – they have called it a primitive land variety, Nap Hal – this is a wheat variety from India – that they have now protected these wheat varieties, Indian farmers who have grown these wheat for centuries are in danger of being punished for the product piracy. At the same time, Monsanto was able to patent the entire chain of goods from the field to the plate. That is to say, it is not only about wheat, it is also about flour, it is about the final product, and Monsanto can decide for himself what supermarket chains and what price the products can be sold in. After it has to be expected that the share of goods protected by intellectual property rights in international trade will increase from 10 to 20 percent to 60 to 80 percent in the coming years, we must also assume that we will find more and more cases of this in Austria. According to the 2001 Product Piracy Act, 613 cases have been treated so far, 56 of which have been passed on to the owners without objection, 12 cases are pending, and 359 cases have been treated in the context of a simplified procedure, which means that the goods have been destroyed. This leaping rise causes us to see the public hand increasingly need to protect rights demanded by companies that have their headquarters in North America or even in the Cayman Islands. My concern is: Step-by-step Adjustments made to Austrian laws, and the WTO TripS Agreement is being implemented in the Austrian legal system through the back door. In the Austrian Parliament and in the EU Parliament, profound changes are no longer debated at all and are therefore not subject to any democratic process in legitimate bodies, but simply questionably taken over by the WTO. I think it is high time that we here in this House review all existing international conventions – UPOV, CBD, IU, Trips, EPC, European Patent Agreement –, see if they are at all compatible, and then find a suitable legal regime for us, for Austria.
